314:                                 re.sub(r"\([^)]*\)", " ", item), maxsplit=1)[0].strip()
315:                 # an unlinkable name ("Tianjin Medical University ...") fills nothing;
316:                 # the item itself is already verified
317:                 linked = RE_NCT.search(lead) or any(
318:                     e.get("type") in ("Drug", "Target", "Company") for e in linker(lead))
319:                 fill = lead if linked else ""
320:                 _check_unit(_join(fill, heading), statements, linker, st, flag,
321:                             joined=bool(fill))
322:             st.note(item_ents)
323:             continue
324:         _check_unit(unit, statements, linker, st, flag)
325:     return findings
326: 
327: 
328: def _check_unit(unit, statements, linker, st, flag, lenient=False, joined=False):
329:     toks, ents = _tokenise(unit, linker)
330:     for clause in _clauses(toks):
331:         # pronouns take the subject as it stands at this clause, not at the unit start;
332:         # one after a relation verb is that relation's object ("targets EGFR and
333:         # inhibits it"); a plural one stands for every member of the subject group
334:         plural = None
335:         for k, t in enumerate(clause):
336:             if t[0] != "ENT" or ents[t[1]]["type"] != "Pronoun":
337:                 continue
338:             p = ents[t[1]]
339:             want = p["want"]
340:             back = [x for x in clause[max(0, k - 2):k] if x[0] == "W"]
341:             if want is None and back and back[-1][1] in set(RELATION) | {"on", "of"}:
342:                 verb = next((x[1] for x in reversed(back) if x[1] in RELATION), None)
343:                 if verb:
344:                     want = ROLES[RELATION[verb][0]][1]
345:             if p["label"] in PLURAL and want is None and len(st.group) > 1:
346:                 plural = (t[1], list(st.group))
347:                 ents[t[1]] = dict(st.group[0])
348:                 continue
349:             ante = st.by_type.get(want) if want else st.subject
350:             ents[t[1]] = (dict(ante, _pron=True) if ante
351:                           else {"type": "Missing", "label": p["label"]})
352:         if plural:
353:             idx, members = plural
354:             for m in members:                 # "they are approved": each of them
355:                 ents[idx] = dict(m)
356:                 _check_clause(clause, ents, statements, st, flag, lenient, joined)
357:         else:
358:             _check_clause(clause, ents, statements, st, flag, lenient, joined)
359: 
360: 
361: def _check_clause(clause, ents, statements, st, flag, lenient=False, joined=False):
362:     if True:
363:         c_ents = [ents[t[1]] for t in clause if t[0] == "ENT"]
364:         words = [t[1] for t in clause if t[0] == "W"]
365:         has_num = any(t[0] == "N" for t in clause)
366: 
367:         refused = [e for e in c_ents if e["type"] == "Refused"]
368:         for e in refused:
369:             flag("distinct_product", e["label"], "names a distinct product (biosimilar, "
370:                  "other formulation, tracer) the graph does not hold")
371:         c_ents = [e for e in c_ents if e["type"] != "Refused"]
372: 
373:         gone = [e for e in c_ents if e["type"] == "Missing"]
374:         if len(gone) == len(c_ents) and not has_num and all(
375:                 w in META_OK or w.endswith("n't") or w in FUNCTION
376:                 or (w in KIND_WORDS and META_ANCHOR & set(words)) for w in words) and (
377:                 not any(w in KIND_WORDS or w in FUNCTION for w in words if w not in META_OK)
378:                 or META_ANCHOR & set(words)):
379:             return                            # "this graph cannot answer that"
380:         if gone:
381:             flag("no_antecedent", gone[0]["label"],
382:                  "a pronoun with nothing before it to refer to")
383:             return
384: 
385:         pred = next((i for i, t in enumerate(clause)
386:                      if t[0] == "W" and t[1] in PREDICATE), len(clause))
387:         implicit = None
388:         if pred < len(clause) and not lenient and not any(
389:                 t[0] == "ENT" and ents[t[1]]["type"] != "Refused" for t in clause[:pred]):
390:             implicit = st.subject             # "... and is phase 3"
391:         subjects = c_ents + ([implicit] if implicit and implicit not in c_ents else [])
392:         if not subjects:
393:             content = [w for w in words if w not in FUNCTION and w not in BE]
394:             if content or has_num:
395:                 flag("no_subject", " ".join(content[:6]) or "figure",
396:                      "this clause names no entity the evidence can be checked against")
397:             return
398:         labels = [e["label"].lower() for e in subjects]
399:         support = [(r, s) for r, s in statements if all(_has(l, s) for l in labels)]
400:         covered = _check_relations(clause, ents, implicit, statements, flag, lenient)
401:         if covered is None and pred < len(clause) and len(
402:                 {e["label"].lower() for e in c_ents}) > 1:
403:             # "PLB1003 is LORLATINIB": two entities joined by a verb that is no relation
404:             flag("identity", " = ".join(e["label"] for e in c_ents),
405:                  "two different entities joined by a verb the graph has no edge for")
406:         # under a joined heading the first entity is the copied line's subject and the
407:         # dash is ours; the heading's own named entities still may not pair silently
408:         own = ([ents[t[1]] for t in clause if t[0] == "ENT"][1:] if joined else c_ents)
409:         own = [e for e in own if not e.get("_pron")]
410:         if covered is None and pred == len(clause) and not lenient and (
411:                 not joined or own):
412:             kinds = {e["type"] for e in c_ents}
413:             first = next((t for t in clause if t[0] != "P"), None)
414:             if len(kinds) > 1 or (first and first[0] == "W" and first[1] in
415:                                   ("on", "of", "to", "in", "with", "for", "against", "by")):
416:                 flag("fragment", " ".join(e["label"] for e in c_ents) or first[1],
417:                      "a clause with no verb: the relation it implies cannot be read")
418:         if covered is not None and not lenient:
419:             for t in clause:
420:                 if t[0] == "ENT" and t[1] not in covered and ents[t[1]]["type"] != "Refused":
421:                     flag("unrelated_entity", ents[t[1]]["label"],
422:                          "named in a clause that states a relation, but not part of any "
423:                          "relation that was checked")
424:         _check_attributes(clause, ents, subjects, support, statements, flag)
425:         group = [ents[t[1]] for t in clause[:pred] if t[0] == "ENT"]
426:         st.note(c_ents, group)
427: 
428: 
429: def _tokenise(unit, linker):
430:     """Entities and pronouns become placeholders."""
431:     s = unit
432:     for pat in SCOPE_PHRASES:
433:         s = re.sub(pat, " nsclc ", s, flags=re.I)
434:     s = s.replace("(s)", "s").replace("’", "'")
435:     s = re.sub(r"[()\[\]{}\"*—–]", " ", s)
436:     ents: list[dict] = []
437:     spans = []
438:     for m in RE_NCT.finditer(s):
439:         spans.append((m.start(), m.end(), {"type": "Trial", "label": m.group(0).upper()}))
440: 
441:     def blanked():
442:         b = list(s)
443:         for a, z, _ in spans:
444:             b[a:z] = " " * (z - a)
445:         # the linker strips only " ?.," from tokens, so "EGFR;" or "ALK:" would not link
446:         return re.sub(r"[;:!]", " ", "".join(b))
447: 
448:     # the linker reports each entity once, so "Osimertinib (Tagrisso)" needs a second look
449:     for _ in range(8):
450:         new = 0
451:         for e in linker(blanked()):
452:             if e.get("type") == "Disease":
453:                 continue
454:             words = [re.escape(w) for w in (e.get("matched_text") or "").split()]
455:             if not words:
456:                 continue
457:             pat = r"(?<![A-Za-z0-9])" + r"[\W_]+".join(words) + r"(?![A-Za-z0-9])"
458:             for m in re.finditer(pat, s, re.I):
459:                 if not any(a < m.end() and m.start() < b for a, b, _ in spans):
460:                     spans.append((m.start(), m.end(), e))
461:                     new += 1
462:         if not new:
463:             break
464:     low = s.lower()
465:     for phrase, typ in sorted(PRONOUN.items(), key=lambda x: -len(x[0])):
466:         for m in re.finditer(rf"\b{re.escape(phrase)}\b", low):
467:             if any(a < m.end() and m.start() < b for a, b, _ in spans):
468:                 continue
469:             after = low[m.end():]
470:             if re.match(r"\s+of\b", after) or any(
471:                     a >= m.end() and not s[m.end():a].strip(" ,") for a, _, _ in spans):
472:                 continue                 # "the target of X", "the drug osimertinib"
473:             spans.append((m.start(), m.end(), {"type": "Pronoun", "label": phrase,
474:                                                "want": typ}))
475:     spans.sort()
476:     out, last = [], 0
477:     for a, b, e in spans:
478:         out.append(s[last:a])
479:         out.append(f" \x00{len(ents)}\x00 ")
480:         ents.append(e)
481:         last = b
482:     out.append(s[last:])
483:     toks = []
484:     for m in RE_TOKEN.finditer("".join(out).lower()):
485:         if m.group(1) is not None:
486:             toks.append(("ENT", int(m.group(1))))
487:         elif m.group(0) in ",;:":
488:             toks.append(("P", m.group(0)))
489:         elif m.group(0)[0].isdigit() and not re.fullmatch(r"\d+[a-z]+", m.group(0)):
490:             toks.append(("N", m.group(0).replace(",", "").rstrip("%")))
491:         else:
492:             toks.append(("W", m.group(0)))
493:     return toks, ents
494: 
495: 
496: def _clauses(toks):
497:     """Split at ; and at and/but/while/whereas when both sides have a predicate."""
498:     joins = ("and", "but", "while", "whereas")
499:     cuts = [-1]
500:     for i, t in enumerate(toks):
501:         if t == ("P", ";"):
502:             cuts.append(i)
503:         elif t[0] == "W" and t[1] in joins:
504:             left = toks[cuts[-1] + 1:i]
505:             nxt = next((j for j in range(i + 1, len(toks))
506:                         if (toks[j][0] == "W" and toks[j][1] in joins)
507:                         or toks[j] == ("P", ";")), len(toks))
508:             right = toks[i + 1:nxt]
509:             if any(x[0] == "W" and x[1] in PREDICATE for x in left) and \
510:                     any(x[0] == "W" and x[1] in PREDICATE for x in right):
511:                 cuts.append(i)
512:     cuts.append(len(toks))
513:     return [toks[a + 1:b] for a, b in zip(cuts, cuts[1:]) if toks[a + 1:b]]
514: 
515: 
516: def _group(clause, k, step, skip=()):
517:     """Positions of the entity run nearest to k in direction `step`."""
518:     i = k + step
519:     while 0 <= i < len(clause) and (clause[i][0] != "ENT" or i in skip):
520:         i += step
521:     grp = []
522:     while 0 <= i < len(clause):
523:         if clause[i][0] == "ENT" and i not in skip:
524:             grp.append(i)
525:             i += step
526:         elif clause[i] in GROUP_GLUE:
527:             i += step
528:         elif step > 0 and grp and clause[i - 1] == ("W", "and"):
529:             # an elided repeat: "... of 14 trials of A and 10 trials of B" -> A, B
530:             j = i
531:             while j < len(clause) and clause[j][0] in ("N", "W") and not (
532:                     clause[j][0] == "W" and clause[j][1] in PREDICATE):
533:                 j += 1
534:             if j < len(clause) and clause[j][0] == "ENT" and j not in skip:
535:                 i = j
536:                 continue
537:             break
538:         else:
539:             break
540:     return grp
541: 
542: 
543: def _is_passive(clause, k):
544:     """A participle right after a form of "be" (adverbs allowed), or before "by"."""
545:     if k + 1 < len(clause) and clause[k + 1] == ("W", "by"):
546:         return True
547:     i = k - 1
548:     while i >= 0 and clause[i][0] == "W" and clause[i][1] in PASSIVE_GAP:
549:         i -= 1
550:     return i >= 0 and clause[i][0] == "W" and clause[i][1] in BE
551: 
552: 
553: def _check_relations(clause, ents, implicit, statements, flag, lenient):
554:     """Check every relation in the clause; return the clause positions it covered,
555:     or None when the clause states no relation."""
507:     when ownership changes.
508: 13. **The claim check reads word order, not meaning.** Generated answers are read
509:     clause by clause into subject, relation and object (`src/claims.py`, rules in
510:     its docstring) and compared with the retrieved evidence for those entities. It
511:     is a set of patterns, so it is only as good as the sentence shapes it was
512:     written for. Three adversarial rounds found 33, about 40 and then about 25
513:     shapes that got through (reversed nouns, clefts, a second object after "and
514:     on", pronouns read too early, plural pronouns, a count borrowed from a phase or
515:     shared by two drugs, `agonist` inside `antagonist`, headings, bracketed text,
516:     refused product names, words like "clinical trials" or "drug" treated as
517:     grammar, "approved in NSCLC"). Each is now a regression test, but a shape
518:     nobody has tried yet can still be misread. Known to get through: a clause whose
519:     words, entities and figures all come from one evidence statement about those
520:     entities but are arranged into a different claim; a drug name outside the
521:     vocabulary that does not look like an INN; odd but evidence-consistent wording
522:     ("osimertinib is the inhibition of EGFR"). Known to be over-strict: a true fact
523:     the graph does not hold, a paraphrase it has no word for ("supports trials"), a
524:     sentence about the evidence itself that uses a negation or "develops", and a
525:     refusal naming something outside the graph ("the capital of France"). Three
526:     narrow exceptions are deliberate: a line copied from an evidence statement
527:     passes (its heading is still checked), a clause with no entity that only talks
528:     about the graph passes, and a bracket is dropped as a citation only when every
529:     id in it is a record of the retrieved evidence.
530: 14. **Provenance does not record the code version.** Snapshots carry their
531:     fetch time, but the graph does not store which revision of the rules built
532:     it, so a change that arrives with a new snapshot and a code change together
533:     cannot be separated from the database alone; only git history can.
534: 8. **The query layer answers the nearest question it can.** "Is pembrolizumab
535:    safe in patients with autoimmune disease?" returns a drug profile rather than
536:    refusing. It refuses only when no entity links at all.
537: 9. **`MEDI4736` stays ambiguous** although it is unambiguously durvalumab,
538:    because the contaminated synonym blocks the preferred-name rule.
539: 10. **A two-word personal name is indistinguishable from a short company name.**
540:     `Xiuning Le` is a person and `Shanghai Henlius` is a company; only an
541:     explicit credential (`MD`, `PhD`) is used to separate them, so the 36
542:     `Investigator` nodes are the credentialed ones and the rest stay `Company`.
543:     An earlier shape-based guess typed Accuray and Daiichi Sankyo as people.
544: 
545: ---

    @staticmethod
    def check_groundedness(text: str, evidence: list[Evidence],
                           entities: list[dict], generated: bool = True,
                           vocabulary: set | None = None, linker=None) -> dict:
        """Report what in a *generated* answer the retrieved evidence does not support.

        Two things this check is not, both found by an adversarial review and
        written down here rather than papered over:

        1. It is meaningless on the template answer. The template is built by
           concatenating the evidence statements, so checking it against those
           same statements is circular -- it cannot fail, and reporting
           `grounded=True` for it would be a test that always passes. When the
           answer was rendered rather than generated, the check reports
           `applicable=False` and asserts nothing.
        2. It is a reader of word order, not of meaning. Without a `linker` it
           only checks that capitalised entities, drug-shaped names and numbers
           appear somewhere in the retrieved set. With one (the query layer's
           entity linker) it also reads each clause into subject, relation and
           object (claims.check_claims) and reports relations, values and words
           the evidence does not give for those entities. Sentence shapes it was
           not written for can still be misread; claims.py and README known
           defect 13 list what is known to get through.
        """
        if not generated:
            return {
                "grounded": None,
                "applicable": False,
                "unsupported_claims": [],
                "evidence_count": len(evidence),
                "method": "not applicable",
                "why": ("the answer was rendered directly from the retrieved "
                        "evidence, so checking it against that evidence is "
                        "circular and would always pass"),
            }

        haystack = " ".join(
            [e.statement for e in evidence]
            + [str(v) for e in evidence for v in e.detail.values()]
            + [e.source_record or "" for e in evidence]
            + [en["label"] for en in entities]
        ).lower()
        # Words are tokenised so "47" is not satisfied by NCT02303977. Numbers
        # are tokenised separately and whole, decimals included: splitting on
        # non-alphanumerics turned evidence "47.5" into the tokens "47" and
        # "5", so an invented "47" passed and the exact "47.5" was rejected.
        haystack_tokens = set(re.findall(r"[a-z0-9]+", haystack))
        haystack_numbers = {n.replace(",", "") for n in RE_NUMBER.findall(haystack)}

        unsupported: list[dict] = []

        def alias_in_evidence(word: str) -> bool:
            if linker is None:
                return False
            return any(e.get("type") in ("Drug", "Target", "Company")
                       and e["label"].lower() in haystack for e in linker(word))

        # "phase 3" is stated as PHASE3 in the evidence, so a phase number is
        # matched against that form, not against the bare figures
        haystack_flat = re.sub(r"[\s_\-]+", "", haystack)
        phase_nums = {n for m in re.finditer(r"\bphase\s*(\d)(?:\s*/\s*(\d))?\b", text, re.I)
                      for n in m.groups() if n}
        # digits inside a linked name ("AZD 9291") are part of the name, not a figure
        number_text = text
        if linker is not None:
            for _ in range(8):           # the linker reports each entity once
                before = number_text
                for e in linker(number_text):
                    mt = e.get("matched_text") or ""
                    if mt and e.get("type") != "Refused" and re.search(r"\d", mt):
                        number_text = re.sub(r"[\W_]+".join(map(re.escape, mt.split())),
                                             " ", number_text, flags=re.I)
                if number_text == before:
                    break
        for num in {n.rstrip(",") for n in RE_NUMBER.findall(number_text)}:
            if num in phase_nums and (linker is not None or f"phase{num}" in haystack_flat):
                continue      # with a linker, claims.py checks the phase per entity
            if num.replace(",", "") not in haystack_numbers:
                unsupported.append({"kind": "number", "value": num,
                                    "why": "not present in the retrieved evidence"})

        # A figure written in words cannot be matched against the evidence, so
        # it is reported as unverifiable rather than allowed through.
        for word in set(RE_NUMBER_WORD.findall(text.lower())):
            unsupported.append({"kind": "number_in_words", "value": word,
                                "why": "a figure written in words cannot be "
                                       "checked against the evidence"})

        capitalised = set(re.findall(r"\b[A-Z][A-Za-z0-9\-]{3,}\b", text))
        stop = {"Scope", "Organisations", "Trials", "Profile", "Findings",
                "Agents", "Reported", "This", "The", "None", "NSCLC", "What",
                "There", "These", "That", "Those", "According", "Sponsors"}
        for name in capitalised - stop:
            if name.lower() not in haystack and not alias_in_evidence(name):
                unsupported.append({"kind": "entity", "value": name,
                                    "why": "named in the answer but not in the evidence"})

        # Drug names are usually lowercase in prose ("xyzabumab cures cancer"
        # passed the capitalised-only check). Anything shaped like an INN or a
        # development code must appear in the evidence too.
        for name in set(RE_DRUGLIKE.findall(text)):
            if (name.lower() not in haystack_tokens and name.lower() not in haystack
                    and not alias_in_evidence(name)):
                unsupported.append({"kind": "drug_like_name", "value": name,
                                    "why": "looks like a drug name or code but "
                                           "is not in the evidence"})

        # Any word the drug vocabulary knows ("aspirin") is a drug name,
        # whatever its shape, and must be in the evidence.
        if vocabulary:
            for w in set(re.findall(r"[A-Za-z][A-Za-z0-9\-]{3,}", text)):
                if (T.canonical_key(w) in vocabulary and w.lower() not in haystack
                        and not alias_in_evidence(w)):
                    unsupported.append({"kind": "drug_name", "value": w,
                                        "why": "a known drug name not in the evidence"})

        # Figures in forms the number matcher does not read: scientific
        # notation ("1e6") and Unicode fractions or superscripts ("⅔").
        for odd in set(RE_ODD_NUMBER.findall(text)):
            unsupported.append({"kind": "number_unreadable", "value": odd,
                                "why": "a figure in a form the check cannot match"})

        if linker is not None:
            from claims import check_claims
            unsupported.extend(check_claims(text, evidence, linker, entities))

        # one finding per value, first reason kept, in a stable order
        seen_values: set[str] = set()
        key = lambda u: (str(u["value"]), u.get("sentence", ""))
        unsupported = [u for u in sorted(unsupported, key=lambda u: (*key(u), u["kind"]))
                       if not (key(u) in seen_values or seen_values.add(key(u)))]

        return {
            "grounded": not unsupported,
            "applicable": True,
            "unsupported_claims": unsupported,
            "evidence_count": len(evidence),
            "method": ("entity and tokenised-numeric overlap against the "
                       "retrieved evidence" + ("; sentence claims checked against "
                       "edges and entity-scoped wording" if linker is not None
                       else "; relations not checked (no linker)")),
        }


RE_ODD_NUMBER = re.compile(r"\d+(?:\.\d+)?[eE][+\-]?\d+|[\u00bc-\u00be\u2150-\u215e\u00b2\u00b3\u00b9\u2070-\u2079]")
RE_NUMBER = re.compile(r"(?<![\w.])\d[\d,]{0,8}(?:\.\d+)?(?![\w])")
RE_NUMBER_WORD = re.compile(
    r"\b(?:zero|one|two|three|four|five|six|seven|eight|nine|ten|eleven|"
    r"twelve|thirteen|fourteen|fifteen|sixteen|seventeen|eighteen|nineteen|"
    r"twenty|thirty|forty|fifty|sixty|seventy|eighty|ninety|hundred|thousand|"
    r"million|half|third|quarter|percent)\b")
# INN stems common in oncology, plus development codes (letters, optional
# hyphen, digits: "AZD9291", "MK-3475", "CC-486")
RE_DRUGLIKE = re.compile(
    r"\b(?:[a-z]{3,}(?:mab|nib|tinib|parib|ciclib|lisib|platin|taxel|rubicin|"

85: `clinical`, `trial(s)`, `drug(s)` and entity-type nouns are in `FUNCTION` (`src/claims.py:104-116`). With an entity they bypass wording validation (`:721-722`); without an entity, the no-subject branch reports nothing when all words are grammar (`:329-334`). The `nsclc` rule permits adjacency to `trials`/`drug(s)` or a preceding `in`, then removes the token without requiring disease evidence (`:690-699`). `Osimertinib is for lung cancer.` correctly fails, but changing the construction to `is an NSCLC drug` passes.
86: 
87: Related unchecked copulas show the fragment rule's limit: Q4 / `PLB1003 is LORLATINIB.` and Q2 / `NCT03909334 is NCT07680790.` both pass. `is` counts as a predicate, so the fragment guard (`:338-344`) does not apply, while it is not a checked relation. No retrieved statement equates these separate entities.

## Reproduction

From the repository root, run `powershell -NoProfile -File audit_evidence_claims/r4/replay4_reproduce.ps1`. It reruns all 14 historical scripts and the 213-test regression command, then rebuilds this report and the input-identity assertions. No historical files are overwritten.
12. **모회사 지도는 날짜가 붙은 주장입니다.** 모회사 키 56개와 같은 회사 키 8개를 분사·사명 변경·두 Merck 같은 뚜렷한
    변화에 대해 확인했지만, 모든 자회사의 법인 존속은 검증하지 않았고 소유가 바뀌어도 다시 확인하는
    장치가 없습니다.
13. **주장 검사는 뜻이 아니라 단어 순서를 읽습니다.** 생성된 답을 절마다 주어·관계·목적어로 읽어
    (`src/claims.py`, 규칙은 docstring) 그 대상들의 근거와 대조합니다. 정해진 문장 모양을 읽는 규칙이라
    처음 보는 모양에는 약합니다. 적대 검증 세 차례에서 33개, 약 40개, 약 25개의 빠져나가는 모양(뒤집힌
    명사형, 강조 구문, "and on" 뒤 두 번째 대상, 너무 일찍 풀린 대명사, 복수 대명사, 단계 숫자나 다른 약의
    숫자를 시험 수로 빌림, antagonist 안의 agonist, 목록 제목, 대괄호 글, 거절된 제품 이름, "clinical
    trials"·"drug"를 문법 단어로 취급, "approved in NSCLC")이 나왔고 모두 회귀 시험이 됐지만, 아직 아무도
    시도하지 않은 모양은 잘못 읽을 수 있습니다. 알려진 통과: 한 대상에 대한 근거 문장 하나의 단어·대상·숫자로
    다른 주장을 짜 맞춘 절, 어휘에 없고 INN 모양도 아닌 약 이름, 근거와 어긋나지 않는 어색한 표현("osimertinib
    is the inhibition of EGFR"). 알려진 과잉 차단: 그래프에 없는 참인 사실, 단어가 없는 바꿔 말하기
    ("supports trials"), 근거 자체를 설명하며 부정·"develops"를 쓴 문장, 그래프 밖 대상을 말하는 거절 문장
    ("the capital of France"). 일부러 둔 예외 세 가지: 근거 문장을 옮긴 줄(그 위 제목은 검사), 대상 없이
    그래프 자체만 말하는 절, 안의 식별자가 모두 검색된 근거의 기록일 때만 지우는 대괄호 인용.
14. **출처 기록에 코드 버전이 없습니다.** 스냅샷은 수집 시각을 갖지만 그래프는 어떤 규칙 버전이
    만들었는지 저장하지 않으므로, 새 스냅샷과 코드 변경이 함께 들어오면 데이터베이스만으로는
    둘을 나눌 수 없고 git 이력으로만 가능합니다.

## 실행

```bash
pip install -r requirements.txt

python src/run_all.py              # 수집 → 해석 → 검증 → 그래프 → 채점 → 렌더

<section>
  <p class="eyebrow">06 &middot; 질문에 답하기</p>
  <h2>근거로만 답하고, 스스로 검사합니다</h2>
  <p class="lede">질문 속 약 이름도 같은 사전으로 맞추기 때문에, 상품명(예: Tagrisso)으로 물어도 표준 번호로 저장된 지도에서 찾습니다.
  답을 만든 뒤에는 <b>찾은 근거에 없는 약 이름이나 숫자가 답에 들어갔는지</b> 검사해서, 있으면 그대로 내보내지 않고 표시합니다.
  관련 없는 질문(예: 프랑스 수도)에는 답하지 않습니다.</p>
  <div class="plain"><b>어디까지 잡나</b> &mdash; AI가 쓴 답을 절마다 주어&middot;관계&middot;목적어로 읽어, 관계는 말한 방향대로 같은 두 대상 사이의 근거가 있는지,
  단계&middot;숫자는 그 값이 속한 대상의 근거에 있는지 봅니다. &ldquo;안전하다&rdquo;, &ldquo;중단했다&rdquo;, &ldquo;~하지 않는다&rdquo;, &ldquo;개발한다&rdquo;처럼 그래프가 보증할 수 없는 말도 걸러냅니다.
  다만 뜻이 아니라 단어 순서를 읽는 규칙이라, 처음 보는 문장 모양은 잘못 읽을 수 있습니다 (적대 검증 두 차례에서 찾은 빠져나가는 모양은 모두 회귀 시험으로 막았습니다).
  일부러 엄격하게 만들어 그래프에 없는 사실은 맞는 말이어도 &lsquo;확인 불가&rsquo;로 걸고, 판단은 사람에게 넘깁니다.
  지금 보이는 답은 언어 모델 없이 근거를 정해진 틀로 보여 준 것이라 검사 대상이 아닙니다.</div>
  </section>
  <section>
    <h2>3. AI 답 검사해 보기</h2>
    <p class="sub">질문과, AI가 했을 법한 답을 넣으면 그 질문의 근거와 절마다 대조합니다. 근거에 없는 약 이름·숫자,
    방향이 틀리거나 근거에 없는 관계, 다른 대상의 단계·숫자를 빌려 온 것, 그리고 &ldquo;안전하다&rdquo;, &ldquo;중단했다&rdquo;, &ldquo;~하지 않는다&rdquo;, &ldquo;개발한다&rdquo;처럼
    그래프가 보증할 수 없는 말을 찾아냅니다. 뜻이 아니라 단어 순서를 읽는 규칙이라 처음 보는 문장 모양은 놓칠 수 있고, 일부러 엄격해서 맞는 말이라도 그래프에 없으면 걸립니다.
    걸린 것과 안 걸린 것 모두, 정말 맞는지는 바이오 지식으로 판단해 보세요.</p>
    <form id="f3"><input id="q3" style="width:100%;margin-top:14px" placeholder="질문 (영어)" autocomplete="off" aria-label="질문">
      <textarea id="a3" rows="3" style="width:100%;margin-top:8px;font:inherit;padding:10px 12px;border:1px solid var(--line);border-radius:8px;background:var(--bg);color:var(--ink)" placeholder="AI의 답 (영어)" aria-label="AI의 답"></textarea>
      <div class="row" style="margin-top:8px"><button>검사하기</button></div></form>
  o.innerHTML="검사 중…";
  try{
    const r = await fetch("/api/check",{method:"POST",headers:{"Content-Type":"application/json"},body:JSON.stringify({text:q,answer:a})});
    const j = await r.json(); if(!r.ok) throw new Error(j.error||r.status);
    const c = j.checks, bad = c.unsupported_claims||[];
    o.innerHTML = `<div><span class="chip ${bad.length?"no":"ok"}">${bad.length?`근거에 없는 내용 ${bad.length}개`:"걸린 것 없음"}</span>`+
      ` <span class="code">근거 ${j.evidence_count}개와 대조</span></div>`+
      (bad.length?bad.map(u=>`<div class="comp no"><div class="top"><span class="s">${esc(u.value)}</span><span class="verdict">${esc(KIND[u.kind]||u.kind)}</span></div>${u.sentence?`<div class="detail">문장: ${esc(u.sentence)}</div>`:""}</div>`).join(""):
        `<p class="detail">걸린 것이 없습니다. 이것이 답이 맞다는 증명은 아닙니다 &mdash; 검사는 단어 순서를 읽을 뿐이니 최종 판단은 직접 읽어 보고 하세요.</p>`)+
      (j.evidence.length?`<details><summary>대조한 근거 보기</summary><div class="ev">${j.evidence.map(x=>`<div>• ${esc(x.statement)} — ${esc(x.source_record||x.source)}</div>`).join("")}</div></details>`:"");
  }catch(err){ o.innerHTML=`<p class="err">오류: ${esc(err.message)}</p>`; }
});

# Round 4 independent specification attack findings

Reviewed `2de10d95295e94c24060648ee01a195496df7acb`, `src/claims.py` changes from `8d4a6060c6d48e6d1d20a2d1470238539bb8fcf9`. All checks used the real graph via `Engine().check(question, answer)`; all new files are under `audit_evidence_claims/r4/`; bytecode generation was disabled. No tracked files changed. `spec_probe.py`, `spec_probe2.py`, `spec_probe3.py`, and `spec_controls.py` reproduce all 54 cases and write same-name JSON. Run from repository root with `PYTHONDONTWRITEBYTECODE=1` and `PYTHONUTF8=1`.

## Specification-axis summary (under 400 words)

The exact R3 repairs do not satisfy the approval requirement: "no known way to make an unsupported relation, value, negation or ordinary-word clinical claim pass ... not already listed as a known residue in README item 13." Five mechanisms remain: plural-group state is lost after one expansion; object-plural pronouns select only the last matching entity; `trials: N` bypasses `count_ambiguous`; a shared phase or status validates just one trial; and a clinical-trial assertion borrows trial existence from another drug via a shared target. Exact inputs below all returned `grounded=True`, `applicable=True`, `unsupported_claims=[]`. The first four require multiple evidence statements; the clinical case adds the absent word `clinical`. Therefore these are outside the specific one-evidence-statement rearrangement residue.

The identity rule also rejects ordinary faithful coordinated statements, and count ambiguity mistakes two aliases of one drug for two drugs. Their individually stated equivalents pass. These are SHOULD FIX, without claiming most faithful outputs fail. The trial-noun and listed-trial relation edges themselves correctly reject tested wrong-drug controls; it is their shared attributes that escape. Evidence-consistent passes and the NSCLC-only rearrangement are NOTES, not independent blockers. No scope creep is asserted.

## BLOCKERS

Question aliases used below are exact:

- Q2: `What trials evaluate Tagrisso?`
- Q4: `Which drugs act on ALK?`
- Q5: `What is AstraZeneca running in NSCLC?`

Every blocker answer below returned `grounded=True`, `applicable=True`, `unsupported_claims=[]`.

### B1. Plural antecedents are lost after one successful expansion

Q4:

`PLB1003 and LORLATINIB act on ALK. they act on ALK. they are approved.`

PLB1003 has max stage PHASE_1, while LORLATINIB has APPROVAL. The first plural clause checks both, but each call to `_check_clause` invokes `st.note` (`src/claims.py:352-356`, `425-426`), replacing the group with the current singleton (`264-270`). The last pronoun checks only LORLATINIB. Reversing the initial order rejects `wording: approved`; capitalizing `They` happens to trigger an unrelated global entity false positive. Source: `spec_probe2.json`, `plural_chain_low`, `plural_chain_lowreverse`; capitalization controls in `spec_probe.json`.

### B2. Object-plural pronouns collapse a multi-drug claim to the last drug

Q5:

`AstraZeneca sponsors osimertinib and carboplatin. It sponsors them in 10 trials.`

Also passes with `AstraZeneca` instead of `It` in the second sentence. Retrieved sponsorship counts are 14 for OSIMERTINIB and 10 for CARBOPLATIN. `them` means the previously listed drugs, yet at `src/claims.py:340-350` its relation-object role sets `want='Drug'`, preventing plural expansion; `st.by_type` stores only the last drug. Thus the count and relation check only CARBOPLATIN. Replacing `them` by `osimertinib` rejects `number: 10`. Sources: `spec_probe.json`, `object_plural_count`; `spec_probe2.json`, `count_object_plural_sentence`, `count_object_plural_singular`.

### B3. Number-after-label counts escape the new ambiguity guard

Q5:

`AstraZeneca sponsors osimertinib and carboplatin in trials: 10.`

Also passes with `clinical trials: 10`. The actual 14 osimertinib trials preclude ten trials covering the combined claim; no joint trial count is provided. At `src/claims.py:798-802`, the numeric checker recognizes a preceding `trials` word, but the new ambiguity guard at `819-820` recognizes only a following trial word. Numeric ownership selects the last drug (CARBOPLATIN) and permits 10. `... in 10 trials.` correctly rejects `count_ambiguous`; `... in trials: 14.` rejects `number: 14`; correct separate 14/10 counts pass. Sources: `spec_probe2.json`, `count_label_shared_same`; `spec_probe3.json`, `trialcount_subjectgroup`; `spec_controls.json`.

### B4. Shared trial phase/status checks just one trial

Q2, each exact answer passes:

1. `NCT03909334 and NCT07680790 are recruiting trials of osimertinib.`
2. `NCT03909334 and NCT07680790 are phase 3 trials of osimertinib.`
3. `Recruiting trials evaluating osimertinib include NCT07680790 and NCT03909334.`
4. `Phase 3 trials evaluating osimertinib include NCT07680790 and NCT03909334.`
5. `NCT07680790 and NCT03909334 evaluate osimertinib and are recruiting.`
6. `NCT07680790 and NCT03909334 evaluate osimertinib and are phase 3.`

NCT03909334 is PHASE2, ACTIVE_NOT_RECRUITING. NCT07680790 is PHASE3, RECRUITING. Trial noun/list relation checks correctly cover both trials (`src/claims.py:559-575`, `651-665`), suppressing the unrelated identity rejection. Phase ownership is one nearest trial (`779-785`); status ownership is one nearest trial (`848-851`). Prefix modifiers select the first listed trial and post-group modifiers the last. Elided predicate variants inherit only `st.subject` (`388-391`), losing the group. Swapping list order in examples 3/4 correctly rejects the status/phase. Single-trial false controls also reject. Sources: `spec_probe2.json`, `status_group_trialof`, `phase_group_trialof`, `status_group_evalafter_rev`, `phase_group_evalafter_rev`; `spec_probe3.json`, `status_list_prefix`, `phase_list_prefix` and their `_control` cases; `spec_controls.json`.

### B5. Trial existence is pooled through a shared target

Q4:

`PLB1003 acts on ALK in clinical trials.`

PLB1003 evidence explicitly says `0 NSCLC trial(s) in this graph`. No Q4 retrieved statement includes the word `clinical`. `wide` selects statements naming ANY entity, so the target ALK pulls in trial counts for other drugs (`src/claims.py:743-758`). `about_trials` then licenses `clinical` and `trials` (`901-907`). The ordinary-word claim that PLB1003 acts on ALK in clinical trials is unsupported by the retrieved evidence. `PLB1003 has clinical trials.` rejects both words, showing the shared-target leakage. Source: `spec_probe3.json`, `clinical_target_pool`, `clinical_target_pool_noedge`; `spec_controls.json`, `clinical_0_only`.

## SHOULD FIX

### S1. Identity rule mistakes coordinated assertions for equivalence

Exact faithful examples:

| Question | Answer | Observed |
|---|---|---|
| Q2 | `NCT07680790 and NCT02411448 are phase 3.` | False; `identity: NCT07680790 = NCT02411448` |
| Q2 | `NCT07680790 and NCT07738172 are recruiting.` | False; `identity: NCT07680790 = NCT07738172` |
| Q4 | `LORLATINIB and CRIZOTINIB are approved.` | False; `identity: LORLATINIB = CRIZOTINIB`, `wording: approved` |

The evidence supports both members in every row. `src/claims.py:401-405` reports every multi-entity non-relation predicate as identity, even when both entities are coordinated subjects. Splitting into two sentences passes (`spec_controls.json`); all grouped failures are in `spec_probe3.json`.

### S2. Count ambiguity mistakes aliases for different drugs

Q5: `AstraZeneca sponsors 14 trials of osimertinib (Tagrisso).`

Observed False; `count_ambiguous: OSIMERTINIB / OSIMERTINIB`. Without `(Tagrisso)` it passes. `src/claims.py:818-829` counts token positions rather than distinct drug IDs/labels. Source `spec_probe3.json`, `faithful_count_alias`, and `spec_controls.json`, `count_alias_control`.

## NOTES

- `Trials evaluating osimertinib include NCT03909334 and NCT07680790.` and `NCT03909334 is a phase 2 trial of osimertinib.` pass, correctly. Corresponding carboplatin claims fail. This isolates B4 to attribute coverage, not failure to compare listed trial-drug relation endpoints.
- `PLB1003 acts on ALK in NSCLC trials.` also passes. Because every content token can come from PLB1003's own statement, this plausibly fits README item 13's one-statement rearrangement residue and is NOT an independent blocker. B5 deliberately uses the additional, absent `clinical` word.
- Evidence-consistent singular object pronoun `Osimertinib acts on EGFR. Osimertinib inhibits it.` passes; no defect asserted.
- Controls are diagnostic, not an estimate of production answer failure rates. No web sources are needed: support here is exclusively the retrieved graph, not external clinical truth.

# Round 4 documentation / headings / kind-word review

HEAD: `2de10d95295e94c24060648ee01a195496df7acb`. Read-only source review; new scripts/results only under `audit_evidence_claims/r4/docs*`. All executions set `PYTHONDONTWRITEBYTECODE=1`, `PYTHONUTF8=1`; scripts also set `sys.dont_write_bytecode=True`. No web search or medical inference was used: support means the actual retrieved graph evidence. No separate tracked AGENTS/CONTRIBUTING/CODING_STANDARDS document was found. User's no-source-edit restriction overrides setup work; no source repairs or external-reference comparison was necessary.

## Standards / documentation axis summary

No standalone violation of a repository coding standard is asserted. Documentation improved: `src/claims.py:10-11` explicitly describes rules rather than a guarantee; README item 13 describes unseen-shape failures and specific known passes/false rejections; `src/query.py:448-466`, `web/template.html:263-267`, `web/try.html:69-72,142-145` qualify the checker and distinguish nothing flagged from proof of truth.

The remaining issue is the requested **explicit known-residue approval bar**, not the old universal-guarantee criticism. `README.md:518-529`, `README.ko.md:360-365` do not list deletion of an unsupported adjective from a heading, kind claims borrowing a different entity's type, or a new clinical-trials claim borrowing evidence from other drugs via a shared target. The examples below are outside the named one-statement-rearrangement residue. A heading is technically sent to the checker, but its critical word has already been erased; the statement that headings are checked (`claims.py:37-39`, `README.md:526-527`) therefore does not describe this loss. Correct these paths or list these precise known residues before claiming the requested approval threshold.

The docstring's general nearest-entity phase description (`claims.py:22-24`) should also be aligned with the deliberate nearest-*trial* preference at `claims.py:779-780`; this is a wording precision issue, not an independent blocker. The web template still describes two adversarial rounds (`web/template.html:265`), while README documents three. Its generic caveat is present and useful.

## BLOCKERS

### D-B1: Heading substitution discards arbitrary clinical assertions

Exact question: `What does osimertinib target?`

Every answer below returns `grounded=True`, `applicable=True`, `unsupported_claims=[]`:

```text
The following safe drugs:
- OSIMERTINIB acts on EGFR (inhibitor).
```

```text
The following discontinued drugs:
- OSIMERTINIB acts on EGFR (inhibitor).
```

```text
The following approved drugs:
- Osimertinib inhibits EGFR.
```

```text
These safe drugs:
- Osimertinib
```

The only retrieved statement is `OSIMERTINIB acts on EGFR (inhibitor)`. None supports safe, discontinued, or approved. These content words do not come from that statement and thus are outside the README residue.

Cause: `src/claims.py:250-256` matches `these|the following|following` plus **any optional alphabetic word** and replaces the entire matched span with the item. It therefore removes `safe`, `discontinued`, or `approved` before tokenization. Both ordinary joined units (`:296`) and copied-line heading checks (`:319-321`) use this function. The new non-lenient check and joined-dash guard cannot inspect words already deleted.

Status example, exact question `What trials evaluate Tagrisso?`:

```text
The following terminated trials:
- NCT07680790 (PHASE3, RECRUITING) evaluates OSIMERTINIB.
```

Also returns True/[] although that trial's evidence says RECRUITING. `The following nonrecruiting trials:` with the same item also passes.

Control with Q3: `safe drugs:\n- OSIMERTINIB acts on EGFR (inhibitor).` returns False with `wording: safe`; `The following drugs:` plus the copied line passes correctly. A heading with its own incompatible entity (`EGFR drugs:` over the copied Q3 line) is rejected as `fragment`. These isolate preprocessing loss from the joined-dash exemption.

Evidence: `docs_probe_results.json` IDs `head_safe`, `head_discontinued`, `head_noninhibitor`, `head_safe_notcopied`, `head_safe_bare`; `docs_controls_results.json` IDs `head_terminated`, `head_safe_explicit`, `head_control_kind`, `head_wrong_entity`; `docs_diagnostics_results.json` IDs `heading_approved`, `heading_nonrecruiting`, `head_lower_control`.

### D-B2: Kind words need any matching entity in the clause, not their actual subject

Exact question: `What does osimertinib target?`

```text
EGFR is a drug inhibited by osimertinib.
```

Observed: True/[]; only evidence is `OSIMERTINIB acts on EGFR (inhibitor)`. EGFR is the target, and the evidence never calls it a drug. The unsupported word `drug` is absent from the sole evidence statement, so this is outside the named residue.

Cause: `src/claims.py:719-720` collects entity kinds anywhere in the clause. `:901-907` accepts `drug` if **any** Drug is present; the valid passive inhibition relation supplies osimertinib, so the incorrect kind assertion about EGFR passes. The identity guard is inapplicable because the clause also contains a recognized relation.

Controls: Q3 `EGFR is a drug.` -> False (`wording: drug`); Q3 `Osimertinib is a drug that inhibits EGFR.` -> True; Q3 `Osimertinib and EGFR are drugs.` -> False (`identity`). Q2 `NCT07680790 is a drug that evaluates osimertinib.` also returns True/[], illustrating the same defect with Trial rather than Target as the misclassified subject.

Evidence: `docs_probe_results.json` ID `kind_drug_target`; `docs_controls_results.json` IDs `kind_control_target`, `kind_target_nonrelation`, `kind_faithful`; `docs_diagnostics_results.json` ID `kind_trial_drug`.

### D-B3: Other drugs' trial evidence supports a novel clinical-trials claim

Exact question: `Which drugs act on ALK?`

```text
PLB1003 inhibits ALK in clinical trials in this graph.
```

Observed: True/[] with all 11 unmodified retrieved evidence statements. PLB1003's own statement says `PLB1003 acts on ALK (inhibitor; max stage PHASE_1; 0 NSCLC trial(s) in this graph)`. The graph provides no PLB1003 trials in this evidence. No Q4 statement contains `clinical`; this new ordinary clinical concept is outside the one-statement vocabulary-rearrangement residue.

Cause: `src/claims.py:745-759` builds `wide` from statements naming **any** clause entity, including ALK. Other ALK drugs have positive trial counts, so `about_trials` becomes true and `:901-907` accepts `clinical`/`trials` for PLB1003. This contradicts the nearby zero-count comment (`:753-754`).

Causal diagnostic: the exact answer checked against only the unchanged PLB1003 evidence row returns False with `wording: clinical` and `wording: trials`. Adding back the other retrieved ALK rows changes it to True. This is an analysis-only subset control; the blocker itself uses Engine.check and the full real retrieval.

Controls: Q4 `PLB1003 is in clinical trials.` -> False; Q4 `Lorlatinib is in clinical trials.` -> True. The omission of ALK removes the unrelated-statement support pool.

Evidence: `docs_probe_results.json` ID `kind_trial_borrow`; `docs_diagnostics_results.json` IDs `trial_graph`, `trial_scope_full`, `trial_scope_plb_only`; `docs_controls_results.json` IDs `kind_control_trial`, `kind_trial_faithful_clinical`.

## SHOULD FIX

### D-S1: A graph-backed target citation is rejected by the older entity-overlap layer

Q3 exact answer: `Osimertinib acts on EGFR [CHEMBL3353410, ENSG00000146648].`

Observed: False; `entity: ENSG00000146648`. Both IDs occur in the retrieved edge ID `CHEMBL3353410|ACTS_ON|ENSG00000146648|CHEMBL3353410`. `claims._strip_citations` correctly removes this bracket, but `src/query.py:533-542` examines capitalized tokens against a haystack assembled at `:482-487` that omits edge IDs. Hence this valid citation fails before the claim check. The single source-record citation `[CHEMBL3353410]` passes.

Whitespace-separated valid IDs are additionally not stripped: `_strip_citations` (`claims.py:211-218`) splits only comma/semicolon while RE_CITE allows whitespace separators. `Osimertinib acts on EGFR [CHEMBL3353410 ENSG00000146648].` returns False with two number findings, an entity finding, and wording `chembl`, `ensg`. This is usability, not a false-acceptance blocker. The documentation says "only when every id" (a necessary condition), so failure to remove valid formats alone does not disprove that sentence.

Evidence: `docs_controls_results.json` IDs `cite_valid`, `cite_valid_list`, `cite_valid_space`; direct transforms in `docs_diagnostics_results.json` (`citation_transform`).

## NOTES / controls

- `Osimertinib [ORG:is-safe] acts on EGFR.` and mixed `[CHEMBL3353410, ORG:is-safe]` both fail; known-ID membership now prevents the earlier identifier-shaped claim deletion. Valid `[CHEMBL3353410]` does not launder explicit `Osimertinib is safe`.
- Q4 `Lorlatinib is approved in NSCLC.`, `Lorlatinib is approved for NSCLC trials.`, and `Lorlatinib is approved for lung cancer.` fail with `indication: lung cancer`. `Lorlatinib is approved.` passes consistently with its APPROVAL stage, so this is a NOTE.
- Q4 `PLB1003 inhibits ALK in NSCLC trials.` passes, but its words can be drawn from PLB1003's own zero-trial statement. Under the requested generous residue boundary, this is a known-residue NOTE, not an independent blocker. D-B3 uses the new word `clinical` and demonstrates cross-row dependence.
- Q5 `AstraZeneca is a drug sponsoring trials of osimertinib.` and `Osimertinib is a company sponsored by AstraZeneca.` also pass. They illustrate kind-owner weakness but are not needed independently; D-B2 has the clearest outside-residue evidence.
- Plain wrong identity `PLB1003 is LORLATINIB.` fails; adding an appositive relation in `PLB1003 is lorlatinib, an ALK inhibitor.` fails as `unrelated_entity: PLB1003`. Same-entity alias `Osimertinib is Tagrisso.` passes consistently.
- Faithful `Osimertinib is evaluated in the clinical trial NCT07680790.` passes. Awkward inputs are not automatically treated as unsupported merely because their prose is odd.
- Template heading source changes are at `src/query.py:403-412`; replay agent owns complete template prose reruns. The heading exploit above does not require an old template or lenient mode.
- `lenient` parameters remain in private signatures, but all production entry paths read here pass the default False. Their presence alone is not a behavioral finding.

Reproduce these focused probes from the repository root: `python audit_evidence_claims/r4/docs_probe.py`, `python audit_evidence_claims/r4/docs_controls.py`, `python audit_evidence_claims/r4/docs_diagnostics.py`, with bytecode suppression. JSON files preserve exact answers, raw check dictionaries and full retrieved evidence (except diagnostics, whose relevant subset rows are included explicitly).

+# Round 4 independent claim-check review
+
+## BLOCKERS
+
+Reviewed HEAD `2de10d95295e94c24060648ee01a195496df7acb` against parent `8d4a6060c6d48e6d1d20a2d1470238539bb8fcf9`, using the real local graph. **Every exact previously reported blocker is now rejected, but new unsupported claims still pass outside README item 13's explicit residues.** Regression tests pass: **213/213**. No tracked files were edited; all review-created files are under `audit_evidence_claims/r4/`.
+
+Question keys below are exact inputs:
+
+| Key | Question |
+|---|---|
+| Q1 | `Which companies are developing osimertinib?` |
+| Q2 | `What trials evaluate Tagrisso?` |
+| Q3 | `What does osimertinib target?` |
+| Q4 | `Which drugs act on ALK?` |
+| Q5 | `What is AstraZeneca running in NSCLC?` |
+| Q6 | `What is the capital of France?` |
+
+All blocker answers below were run through `Engine().check(question, answer)` with unmodified retrieved evidence. **Each returns `grounded=True`, `applicable=True`, `unsupported_claims=[]`.** The standard is support in this retrieval, not external clinical truth. Exact full outputs and evidence are preserved in [root4_confirmation.json](root4_confirmation.json), [root4_probe.json](root4_probe.json), and the linked specialist artifacts. Twenty-five selected outcomes were independently rerun by the root reviewer after the parallel reviews.
+
+### B1. Heading substitution deletes the unsupported clinical assertion
+
+Q3, exact answers (`\n` denotes a newline):
+
+| Answer | Observed |
+|---|---|
+| `The following safe drugs:\n- OSIMERTINIB acts on EGFR (inhibitor).` | True; [] |
+| `The following discontinued drugs:\n- OSIMERTINIB acts on EGFR (inhibitor).` | True; [] |
+| `The following safe drugs:\n- Osimertinib` | True; [] |
+| Control: `These drugs are safe:\n- OSIMERTINIB acts on EGFR (inhibitor).` | False; `wording: safe` |
+
+The only evidence is `OSIMERTINIB acts on EGFR (inhibitor)`. Neither safety nor discontinuation is supported. At `src/claims.py:253-255`, `_join` substitutes `these|the following|following` **plus any optional alphabetic word**. It removes `safe` or `discontinued` before the checker sees the heading. Both ordinary units (`:296`) and copied-line headings (`:319-321`) use this function. Removing lenient mode cannot recover an erased word.
+
+The same defect drops `terminated` from Q2 / `The following terminated trials:\n- NCT07680790 (PHASE3, RECRUITING) evaluates OSIMERTINIB.` The retrieved status is RECRUITING. Wrong-entity joined headings are otherwise rejected, isolating this to substitution rather than the dash exemption. These assertions introduce content absent from the evidence, so the one-statement rearrangement residue does not cover them.
+
+Artifacts: `root4_confirmation.json` IDs `heading_safe`, `heading_discontinued`, `heading_safe_bare`, `heading_safe_control`; [docs_findings.md](docs_findings.md), D-B1 and its exact controls.
+
+### B2. Plural handling still loses members after expansion, in objects, and in elided subjects
+
+| Q | Exact answer | Observed |
+|---|---|---|
+| Q4 | `PLB1003 and LORLATINIB act on ALK and they act on ALK and they are approved.` | True; [] |
+| Q4 | `LORLATINIB and PLB1003 act on ALK and are approved.` | True; [] |
+| Q5 | `AstraZeneca sponsors osimertinib and carboplatin. It sponsors them in 10 trials.` | True; [] |
+| Q4 control | `LORLATINIB and PLB1003 act on ALK and they are approved.` | False; `wording: approved` |
+
+PLB1003 has `max stage PHASE_1`, whereas LORLATINIB has `max stage APPROVAL`. Q5 separately gives AstraZeneca 14 osimertinib trials and 10 carboplatin trials; it does not support the ten-trial assertion about both drugs.
+
+Three related causes:
+
+- Each plural member is checked at `src/claims.py:352-356`, but each check updates shared state at `:425-426`. `_State.note` (`:271-277`) replaces the group with the current singleton. A later `they` sees only the last drug. Reversing the initial drugs in the three-clause example rejects approval. The lowercase multi-sentence equivalent also passes.
+- Object-position `them` receives `want='Drug'` at `:340-344`, bypassing the plural branch's `want is None` condition (`:345`). `:349-350` then uses the last drug stored in `by_type`, CARBOPLATIN. This loses OSIMERTINIB before both relation and count checking.
+- An elided subject uses only `st.subject` (`:388-391`), so `and are approved` checks the first drug, not the coordinated group.
+
+These claims transfer attributes across different drugs and evidence statements; they are not the listed one-statement residue. Artifacts: `root4_confirmation.json` IDs `plural_chain`, `plural_implicit`, `object_count`, `plural_original_fixed`; [spec_findings.md](spec_findings.md), B1/B2.
+
+### B3. `trials: N` bypasses the new count ambiguity check
+
+Q5:
+
+| Exact answer | Observed |
+|---|---|
+| `AstraZeneca sponsors osimertinib and carboplatin in trials: 10.` | True; [] |
+| Control: `AstraZeneca sponsors osimertinib and carboplatin in 10 trials.` | False; `count_ambiguous: OSIMERTINIB / CARBOPLATIN` |
+
+The evidence gives separate 14/10 counts, with no ten-trial count for both drugs. The ordinary number checker recognizes a preceding trial label at `src/claims.py:798-802`, but `count_ambiguous` only looks for a trial word **after** the number (`:819-820`). The count is therefore validated against the final drug alone. Correct separate `14 trials of osimertinib and 10 trials of carboplatin` passes.
+
+Artifacts: `root4_confirmation.json` IDs `colon_count`, `prefix_count_control`; [spec_probe2.json](spec_probe2.json), `count_label_shared_same`; [spec_controls.json](spec_controls.json). This is another cross-statement count transfer, outside the explicit residue.
+
+### B4. A shared phase or status validates only one trial
+
+Q2, each answer returns True; []:
+
+```text
+NCT03909334 and NCT07680790 are recruiting trials of osimertinib.
+```
+
+```text
+NCT03909334 and NCT07680790 are phase 3 trials of osimertinib.
+```
+
+```text
+Recruiting trials evaluating osimertinib include NCT07680790 and NCT03909334.
+```
+
+The evidence is explicit:
+
+```text
+NCT03909334 (PHASE2, ACTIVE_NOT_RECRUITING) evaluates OSIMERTINIB
+NCT07680790 (PHASE3, RECRUITING) evaluates OSIMERTINIB
+```
+
+The new trial-noun/list relation checks cover both endpoints (`src/claims.py:559-575`, `:651-665`), but phase checking selects one nearest trial (`:779-785`) and status checking likewise selects one (`:848-851`). A trailing attribute selects the last trial; a prefix modifier selects the first listed trial. The assertion applies to both. Swapping the IDs in the recruiting-list example changes the result to False (`status: recruiting`). Elided `... evaluate osimertinib and are recruiting` also loses the group.
+
+The original exact R1 group-status input is now rejected by `identity`, not by checking both statuses. Adding the faithful `trials of osimertinib` relation removes that incidental rejection and reveals the same ownership limitation. FIXED in the replay table is consequently an exact-input result, not a claim that the mechanism is safe.
+
+Artifacts: `root4_confirmation.json` IDs `trial_group_status`, `trial_group_phase`, `trial_status_prefix`, `trial_status_swap`; [spec_findings.md](spec_findings.md), B4, including phase-prefix and elided controls. This requires multiple trial statements and is outside the listed residue.
+
+### B5. Kind and clinical words borrow support from the wrong entity
+
+| Q | Exact answer | Observed |
+|---|---|---|
+| Q3 | `EGFR is a drug inhibited by osimertinib.` | True; [] |
+| Q3 control | `EGFR is a drug.` | False; `wording: drug` |
+| Q4 | `PLB1003 inhibits ALK in clinical trials in this graph.` | True; [] |
+| Q4 control | `PLB1003 has clinical trials.` | False; `wording: clinical`, `wording: trials` |
+
+Q3 has only the osimertinib–EGFR inhibitor statement; it never calls EGFR a drug. `present` includes every entity kind anywhere in the clause (`src/claims.py:719-720`), and `:901-907` accepts `drug` whenever any Drug is present. The valid passive relation provides osimertinib and thus licenses a false kind claim about EGFR. The identity rule does not help because the clause contains a relation.
+
+PLB1003's exact evidence is `PLB1003 acts on ALK (inhibitor; max stage PHASE_1; 0 NSCLC trial(s) in this graph)`. No Q4 statement contains `clinical`. The `wide` pool (`:745-759`) includes statements mentioning **any** clause entity, so ALK pulls in other drugs' positive trial counts. `about_trials` then accepts the new clinical-trials claim for PLB1003. A causal diagnostic checks the identical answer against only the unchanged PLB1003 row: it fails both `clinical` and `trials`; the full retrieval passes. The blocker itself uses the full normal retrieval.
+
+These introduce `drug`/`clinical` absent from the relevant evidence and therefore are outside the one-statement vocabulary residue. Artifacts: `root4_confirmation.json` IDs `kind_drug`, `kind_drug_control`, `clinical_trial`, `clinical_trial_control`; [docs_diagnostics_results.json](docs_diagnostics_results.json), `trial_graph`, `trial_scope_full`, `trial_scope_plb_only`; [docs_findings.md](docs_findings.md), D-B2/D-B3.
+
+### B6. Spelling out negation as letters is silently ignored
+
+Q3 / `Osimertinib does N O T inhibit EGFR.` returns True; []. The lower-case `n o t` equivalent also passes. `src/claims.py:484-492` tokenizes the spelling as three single-letter words, and `:908` exempts all words shorter than two characters. This is a deliberately adversarial spelling, less natural than B1–B5; the verdict does not depend on it. It is nevertheless a reproduced unsupported negation, with additional text absent from the evidence and no matching explicit README residue. Ordinary `not`, `non-`, underscore and backtick controls remain rejected.
+
+Artifacts: `root4_confirmation.json`, `split_negation`; `root4_probe.json`, `negation_split`, `non_ascii_hyphen`, `negation_underscore`, `negation_backtick`.

+
+| Q | Exact faithful answer | Observed |
+|---|---|---|
+| Q2 | `NCT07680790 and NCT02411448 are phase 3.` | False; `identity: NCT07680790 = NCT02411448` |
+| Q5 | `AstraZeneca sponsors 14 trials of osimertinib (Tagrisso).` | False; `count_ambiguous: OSIMERTINIB / OSIMERTINIB` |
+| Q4 | `LORLATINIB and CRIZOTINIB act on ALK. They are approved.` | False; `entity: They` |
+| Q3 | `Osimertinib acts on EGFR [CHEMBL3353410, ENSG00000146648].` | False; `entity: ENSG00000146648` |
+| Q2 | `Trials of osimertinib include NCT03909334 and NCT07680790.` | False; `fragment: OSIMERTINIB NCT03909334 NCT07680790` |
+
+Causes and controls:
+
+- `identity` (`src/claims.py:401-405`) treats coordinated subjects plus a shared copula as an equality. Both trials are PHASE3; spelling the statements separately passes.
+- `count_ambiguous` (`:818-829`) counts mention positions, so a repeated alias is treated as another drug. Removing `(Tagrisso)` passes.
+- The global capitalized-name test (`src/query.py:533-542`) has no `They` stop word. Lowercase `they` passes the same supported approval claim.
+- Claim-level citation stripping accepts the comma-separated graph IDs, but the older global haystack (`query.py:482-487`) omits edge IDs and rejects ENSG. A single `[CHEMBL3353410]` citation passes. Space-separated valid IDs additionally mismatch `_strip_citations`' comma/semicolon split (`claims.py:211-218`).
+- The trial-noun rule reads a trial before `of` (`claims.py:563-566`), whereas the faithful noun-list form puts the trials after `include`. The equivalent `Trials evaluating osimertinib include ...` passes.
+
+The original `supports trials` wording and refusals naming France still fail as documented. The six specific R3 S1 repairs all pass. Exact inputs/outputs are in [replay4_report.md](replay4_report.md), [spec_findings.md](spec_findings.md), [docs_findings.md](docs_findings.md), and `root4_confirmation.json` / `root4_probe.json`.
+
+### S2. Documentation improved, but the explicit known-residue list is incomplete
+
+**Documentation/standards axis:** No separate repository coding-standard violation is asserted. The new `claims.py:10-11` explicitly calls its rules a description rather than a guarantee. `README.md:508-529`, `README.ko.md:354-365`, `src/query.py:448-466`, `web/template.html:263-267`, and `web/try.html:69-72,142-145` acknowledge pattern limitations and distinguish no findings from proof of truth. The original blanket documentation criticism should not be repeated unchanged.
+
+However, the explicit known-pass list covers one-statement rearrangement, unknown non-INN drug names and odd evidence-consistent wording. It does not cover erased safety headings, cross-statement plural/count/status transfer, new kind/clinical words admitted through another entity, or ignored spelled-out negation. The broad unseen-shapes caveat is not the explicit known-residue disclosure required by this audit's approval bar. The heading claim is also materially incomplete: `claims.py:37-39` and README say headings are checked, but `_join` has already deleted the critical word in B1. These paths must be repaired or specifically disclosed.
+
+Smaller precision issues: the docstring's nearest-entity phase summary (`claims.py:22-24`) does not reflect the new preference for the nearest **trial** (`:779-781`); `web/template.html:265` still says two adversarial rounds, while README describes three. The documentation-axis assessment is preserved separately in [docs_findings.md](docs_findings.md); the specification-axis assessment is in [spec_findings.md](spec_findings.md).
